   128	def rope_check(model):
   129	    """max |R(d) R(p) x - R(p+d) x| using the model's own apply at p and at p+d,
   130	    and rotate_impl for the increment. Also, on Qwen3, rotate_impl against bi_stream's original."""

     1	# REMEMBER-2: the fair arm. Edits trained WITH history, then measured by length ($30 cap)
     2	
     3	REMEMBER (REMEMBER.md) found pin 80/80 exact at 2,048 in all 10 families, while the same weights with history kept
     4	failed at a median of step 3. A referee will say: "the rank-1 edits were learned in pinned format, so 'remember' is a
     5	format shift, not drift." This run removes that objection. Read REMEMBER.md and UNIVERSAL.md first, and copy the
     6	training code UNIVERSAL used for its rank-1 edits.
     7	
     8	## Design (register in REMEMBER2.md before spending)
     9	- **Models:** 4 families spanning sizes: Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-4B, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B. For each, train **two**
    10	  rank-1 edits with the same recipe, data size and steps as UNIVERSAL:
    11	  - **E-pin:** UNIVERSAL's own edit (reuse it; no retraining);
    12	  - **E-hist:** an edit trained on ticks presented with the full run history in context, positions continuing,
    13	    exactly the "remember" policy. Use training lengths up to 256 events.
    14	- **Evaluate each edit under each policy** (pin, remember) at 256, 1,024, 4,096 and 8,192 events, n = 4 tapes, on the
    15	  same certified tables as REMEMBER. Record exactness, the first-error step and its kind, and the margin trajectory.
    16	- **The questions:**
    17	  - Q1: does E-hist under remember stay exact at its training length (256)? If not, history is a learnability
    18	    problem, not only a format problem.
    19	  - Q2: where does E-hist under remember first fail as length grows? The prediction is drift beyond the training
    20	    length, as BRIDGE saw.
    21	  - Q3: is E-hist still exact under the pin? In other words, does training with history damage the pinned executor?
    22	- **Suggested predictions:**
    23	  - E-hist under remember is mostly exact at 256 and degrades by 4,096–8,192, with first errors growing with
    24	    length rather than at step 3;
    25	  - pin stays exact for both edits.
    26	
    27	## Rules
    28	Use REMEMBER's rules (§6 of REMEMBER_BRIEF.md):
    29	- register before spend; smoke first;
    30	- source-hash guard and redeploy after every edit; fresh adapters per job;
    31	- no typed numbers; recount the three key numbers a second way;
    32	- own app `polyformer-remember2`, own volume `polyformer-remember2-results`;
    33	- record every job in the ledger with GPU and host CPU; cap $30;
    34	- never print keys;
    35	- `nice -n 19`, `/bin/rm`; never block on long-running commands; no local model downloads.

    39	for E-pin, dashed for E-hist). Commit and stop.

    37	def key_length(root):
    38	    plan = plan_of(root)
    39	    if plan is not None:
    40	        return int(plan["key_length"])
    41	    return DEFAULT_KEY_LENGTH

    44	def selected_dirs(root):
    45	    root = pathlib.Path(root)
    46	    plan = plan_of(root)
    47	    ids = {j["id"] for j in plan["jobs"]} if plan is not None else None
    48	    out = []
    49	    for d in sorted(p for p in root.iterdir() if p.is_dir()):
    50	        if ids is not None and d.name not in ids:
    51	            continue
    52	        if ids is None and (d.name.startswith("smoke") or d.name.startswith("_")):
    53	            continue
    54	        if (d / "runs.json").is_file():
    55	            out.append(d)
    56	    return out

    59	def load_payloads(root):
    60	    return [(d.name, json.loads((d / "runs.json").read_text())) for d in selected_dirs(root)]

    63	def keys_from_payloads(payloads, length):
    64	    by_model, steps = {}, []
    65	    exact_excluded = remember_runs = bitwise = ticks = 0
    66	    for _job, payload in payloads:
    67	        if payload.get("cohort") != "main":
    68	            continue
    69	        for run in payload["runs"]:
    70	            if run["policy"] == "remember" and str(length) in run["exact_at"]:
    71	                remember_runs += 1
    72	                exact = int(bool(run["exact_at"][str(length)]))
    73	                by_model.setdefault(payload["model"], []).append(exact)
    74	                if exact == 1:
    75	                    exact_excluded += 1
    76	                else:
    77	                    if run["first_error_step"] is None:
    78	                        raise ValueError(f"inexact remember run with no first error: {payload['model']}")
    79	                    steps.append(int(run["first_error_step"]))
    80	            if run["policy"] == "pin":
    81	                bitwise += int(run["pin_bitwise"])
    82	                ticks += int(run["ticks"])
    83	    return {
    84	        "key_length": length,
    85	        "remember_models_zero_exact": sum(1 for xs in by_model.values() if xs and not any(xs)),
    86	        "remember_median_first_error": lower_median(steps),
    87	        "remember_exact_excluded": exact_excluded,
    88	        "remember_runs": remember_runs,
    89	        "main_models": len(by_model),
    90	        "pin_bitwise": bitwise,
    91	        "pin_ticks": ticks,
    92	    }
    93

    55	            continue
    56	        path = root / job["id"] / "train.json"
    57	        if path.is_file():
    58	            out[job.get("model", job["id"].split("-", 1)[-1])] = json.loads(path.read_text())

    62	def keys_from_runs(runs, key_length, train_length):
    63	    def at(edit, policy, length):
    64	        return [r for r in runs if r["edit"] == edit and r["policy"] == policy and str(length) in r.get("exact_at", {})]
    65	
    66	    rem256 = at("e-hist", "remember", train_length)
    67	    rem_key = at("e-hist", "remember", key_length)
    68	    pin_key = at("e-hist", "pin", key_length)
    69	    steps, excluded = [], 0
    70	    for run in rem_key:
    71	        if run["exact_at"][str(key_length)]:
    72	            excluded += 1
    73	        else:
    74	            if run.get("first_error_step") is None:
    75	                raise ValueError("inexact E-hist remember run has no first error")
    76	            steps.append(int(run["first_error_step"]))
    77	    return {"key_length": int(key_length), "train_length": int(train_length),
    78	            "hist_remember_exact_256": sum(int(bool(r["exact_at"][str(train_length)])) for r in rem256),
    79	            "hist_remember_n_256": len(rem256),
    80	            "hist_remember_median_key": lower_median(steps),
    81	            "hist_remember_exact_excluded_key": excluded,
    82	            "hist_remember_n_key": len(rem_key),
    83	            "hist_pin_exact_key": sum(int(bool(r["exact_at"][str(key_length)])) for r in pin_key),
    84	            "hist_pin_n_key": len(pin_key)}

   368	def splice(text, marker, body):
   369	    start, end = f"<!-- {marker} -->", f"<!-- /{marker} -->"
   370	    i, j = text.index(start), text.index(end)
   371	    return text[:i] + start + "\n\n" + body.strip() + "\n\n" + text[j:]

   374	def require_complete(root, summary):
   375	    plan = plan_of(root)
   376	    if plan is None:
   377	        raise SystemExit("results/plan.json is missing; counted jobs are not registered")
   378	    expect = {f"r-{n}" for n in (*M.MAIN, *M.OLMO)}
   379	    got = {j["id"] for j in plan["jobs"]}
   380	    if got != expect:
   381	        raise SystemExit(f"plan jobs {sorted(got)} != {sorted(expect)}")
   382	    for d in selected_dirs(root):
   383	        payload = json.loads((d / "runs.json").read_text())
   384	        if not payload.get("complete"):
   385	            raise SystemExit(f"{d.name} is not complete")
   386	        if not (d / "runs.csv").is_file():
   387	            raise SystemExit(f"{d.name} has no runs.csv")
   388	    if set(summary["model_order"]) != set(M.MAIN):
   389	        raise SystemExit(f"main models in the records: {summary['model_order']}")
   390	    if set(summary["olmo"]) != set(M.OLMO):
   391	        raise SystemExit(f"olmo models in the records: {sorted(summary['olmo'])}")
   392	    if not (root / "modal_ledger.json").is_file():
   393	        raise SystemExit("modal_ledger.json is missing")

   396	def write(root):
   397	    root = pathlib.Path(root)

   415	def main():
    20	# Duplicated on purpose. An amendment changes results/plan.json, which both counters read.
    21	DEFAULT_KEY_LENGTH = 4096
    22	
    23	
    24	def lower_median(values):
    25	    xs = sorted(values)
    26	    if not xs:
    27	        return None
    28	    return xs[(len(xs) - 1) // 2]
    29	
    30	
    31	def key_length(root):
    32	    plan = pathlib.Path(root) / "plan.json"
    33	    if plan.is_file():
    34	        return int(json.loads(plan.read_text())["key_length"])
    35	    return DEFAULT_KEY_LENGTH
    36	
    37	
    38	def job_ids(root):
    39	    plan = pathlib.Path(root) / "plan.json"
    40	    if not plan.is_file():
    41	        return None
    42	    return {j["id"] for j in json.loads(plan.read_text())["jobs"]}
    43	
    44	
    45	def iter_rows(root):
    46	    root = pathlib.Path(root)
    47	    ids = job_ids(root)
    48	    for d in sorted(p for p in root.iterdir() if p.is_dir()):
    49	        if ids is not None and d.name not in ids:
    50	            continue
    51	        if ids is None and (d.name.startswith("smoke") or d.name.startswith("_")):
    52	            continue
    53	        path = d / "runs.csv"
    54	        if not path.is_file():
    55	            continue
    56	        with path.open() as f:
    57	            yield from csv.DictReader(f)
    58	
    59	
    60	def keys(root):
    61	    """The three numbers. Remember and pin are the main cohort. Exact remember runs are
    62	    left out of the median and counted separately."""
    63	    length = key_length(root)
    64	    by_model = {}
    65	    steps = []
    66	    exact_excluded = 0
    67	    remember_runs = 0
    68	    bitwise = ticks = 0
    69	    for row in iter_rows(root):
    70	        if row["cohort"] != "main":
    71	            continue
    72	        if row["policy"] == "remember" and int(row["length"]) == length:
    73	            remember_runs += 1
    74	            exact = int(row["exact"])
    75	            by_model.setdefault(row["model"], []).append(exact)
    76	            # exact at this length includes a run whose first error is past this prefix
    77	            if exact == 1:
    78	                exact_excluded += 1
    79	            else:
    80	                if row["first_error_step"] == "":
    81	                    raise ValueError(f"inexact remember run with no first error: {row['model']}")
    82	                steps.append(int(row["first_error_step"]))
    83	        if row["policy"] == "pin" and row["pin_bitwise"] != "" and int(row["length"]) == int(row["T"]):
    84	            bitwise += int(row["pin_bitwise"])
    85	            ticks += int(row["pin_ticks"])
    86	    zero = sum(1 for xs in by_model.values() if xs and not any(xs))
    87	    return {
    88	        "key_length": length,
    89	        "remember_models_zero_exact": zero,
    90	        "remember_median_first_error": lower_median(steps),
   395	    i, j = text.index(start), text.index(end)
   396	    return text[:i] + start + "\n\n" + body.strip() + "\n\n" + text[j:]
   397	
   398	
   399	def require_complete(root, summary):
   400	    plan = plan_of(root)
   401	    if plan is None:
   402	        raise SystemExit("results2/plan.json is missing")
   403	    have = {p["model"] for _j, p in selected(root)}
   404	    if have != set(M.MODELS):
   405	        raise SystemExit(f"eval models {sorted(have)} != {list(M.MODELS)}")
   406	    for name in M.MODELS:
   407	        payload = next(p for _j, p in selected(root) if p["model"] == name)
   408	        if set(payload.get("edits", [])) != set(M.EDITS):
   409	            raise SystemExit(f"{name} edits {payload.get('edits')}")
   410	        want = len(M.tables(name)) * int(plan["tapes"]) * len(M.EDITS) * len(M.POLICIES)
   411	        if len(payload["runs"]) != want:
   412	            raise SystemExit(f"{name} has {len(payload['runs'])} runs, expected {want}")
   413	        for run in payload["runs"]:
   414	            if set(int(x) for x in run["exact_at"]) != set(plan["lengths"]):
   415	                raise SystemExit(f"{name} lengths {sorted(run['exact_at'])}")
   416	        train = summary["trains"].get(name)
   417	        if not train:
   418	            raise SystemExit(f"{name} has no train record")
   419	        hist = (payload.get("edit_meta") or {}).get("e-hist") or {}
   420	        if train.get("sha256") != hist.get("sha256"):
   421	            raise SystemExit(f"{name} train sha {train.get('sha256')} != eval sha {hist.get('sha256')}")
   422	    if not (root / "modal_ledger.json").is_file():
   423	        raise SystemExit("modal_ledger.json is missing")
   424	
   425	
   426	def _epin_bits(payloads, key_length):
   427	    pin = [r for _j, p in payloads for r in p["runs"] if r["edit"] == "e-pin" and r["policy"] == "pin"]
   428	    rem = [r for _j, p in payloads for r in p["runs"] if r["edit"] == "e-pin" and r["policy"] == "remember"]
   429	    exact = sum(int(bool(r["exact_at"].get(str(key_length)))) for r in pin)
   430	    steps = [int(r["first_error_step"]) for r in rem if not r["exact_at"].get(str(key_length))]
   431	    return exact, len(pin), lower_median(steps)
   432	
   433	
   434	def write(root):
   435	    root = pathlib.Path(root)
   436	    keys = match_recount(root)
   437	    summary = build(root)
   438	    if summary["keys"] != keys:
   439	        raise SystemExit("build keys diverged from keys_from_dir")
   440	    require_complete(root, summary)
   441	    summary["recount"] = keys
   442	    (root / "summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=1, sort_keys=True, default=str))
   443	    import r2_fig
   444	    r2_fig.main(root / "summary.json")
   445	    md = HERE / "REMEMBER2.md"
   446	    text = md.read_text()
   447	    text = splice(text, "SUMMARY", summary_md(summary))
   448	    text = splice(text, "RESULTS", results_md(summary))
   449	    md.write_text(text)
   450	    print(json.dumps({"wrote": str(md), "keys": keys}))

    38	def run_config(cfg, a, tok, pool, val_tables, out, base_val):
    39	    import torch
    40	    torch.manual_seed(a.seed)
    41	    model = bi_edit.load_base()
    42	    model.gradient_checkpointing_enable(gradient_checkpointing_kwargs={"use_reentrant": False})
    43	    model.enable_input_require_grads()
    44	    model, aux, params = bi_edit.apply_edit(model, cfg["edit"], tok)
    45	    steer = aux.steer
    46	    n_params = bi_edit.count_params(params)
    47	    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(params, lr=cfg["lr"], betas=(0.9, 0.99), weight_decay=0.0) if params else None
    48	    c = bi_edit.core(model)
    49	    pin_rng = random.Random(a.seed + 7919)
    50	    steps = cfg["steps"]
    51	    ckpts = sorted({s for s in cfg.get("ckpts", a.ckpts) if s <= steps} | {steps})
    52	    warm = cfg.get("warmup", a.warmup)
    53	    rec = {"name": cfg["name"], "edit": cfg["edit"], "lr": cfg["lr"], "steps": steps, "warmup": warm,
    54	           "trainable_params": n_params, "log": [], "val": {"0": base_val}, "saved": {}}
    55	    pin_n = a.tables * a.blocks
    56	    t0 = time.time()
    57	    print(json.dumps({"config": cfg["name"], "trainable_params": n_params}), flush=True)
    58	    for step in range(steps):
    59	        lr = cfg["lr"] * min(1.0, (step + 1) / max(1, warm))
    60	        for g in (opt.param_groups if opt else []):
    61	            g["lr"] = lr
    62	        model.train()
    63	        if opt:
    64	            opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
    65	        exs = [pb_pin.pinned_example(pin_rng, pool, "train", a.blocks) for _ in range(a.tables)]
    66	        for ex in exs:
    67	            ex["pi"] = aux.prefix + ex["pi"]
    68	        exs.sort(key=lambda ex: len(ex["pi"]))
    69	        loss_sum, ok = 0.0, 0
    70	        for k in range(0, len(exs), a.micro):
    71	            ids, pos, allow, r, cidx, lab = pb_pin.packed_batch(exs[k:k + a.micro], 0)
    72	            if steer is not None and steer.where == "block":
    73	                P = torch.tensor([len(ex["pi"]) for ex in exs[k:k + a.micro]])[:, None]
    74	                own = torch.arange(ids.shape[1])[None] >= P
    75	                steer.mask = own.cuda()
    76	            h = c.model(input_ids=ids.cuda(), position_ids=pos.cuda(), attention_mask=allow.cuda()).last_hidden_state
    77	            loss, n_ok = ce_sum(c.lm_head, h[r.cuda(), cidx.cuda()], lab.cuda())
    78	            if params:
    79	                (loss / pin_n).backward()
    80	            if steer is not None:
    81	                steer.mask = None
    82	            loss_sum += float(loss.detach())
    83	            ok += n_ok
    84	            del h, loss
    85	        gn = torch.nn.utils.clip_grad_norm_(params, 1.0) if params else torch.zeros(())
    86	        if params:
    87	            opt.step()
    88	        entry = {"step": step + 1, "loss": round(loss_sum / pin_n, 5), "acc": round(ok / pin_n, 5), "lr": lr,
    89	                 "grad_norm": round(float(gn), 4), "elapsed": round(time.time() - t0, 1)}
    90	        rec["log"].append(entry)
    91	        if step % 5 == 0 or step + 1 in ckpts:
    92	            print(json.dumps(entry), flush=True)
    93	        if step + 1 in ckpts:
    94	            model.eval()
    95	            d = out / cfg["name"] / f"step{step + 1}"
    96	            if cfg["edit"].get("kind") == "lora":
    97	                model.save_pretrained(d)
    98	            else:
    99	                aux.save(d)
   100	            rec["saved"][str(step + 1)] = str(d)
   101	            fr = bi_edit.fast_eval(model, pool, val_tables, aux)
   102	            rec["val"][str(step + 1)] = bi_edit.summarize_fast(fr)
   103	            print(json.dumps({"config": cfg["name"], "step": step + 1,
   104	                              "val": {k: rec["val"][str(step + 1)][k] for k in ("accepted", "row_acc", "min_margin_q")}}),
    63	def name_pool(tok, n_train=240, n_test=120):
    64	    """Disjoint train and test names; every name stays one token beside every other name and beside a newline,
    65	    so a row's token ids are exactly (s, e, t, newline) and a block's are (s, e). Each split keeps the kinds'
    66	    proportions of the candidate list."""
    67	    nl = _ids(tok, "\n")
    68	    assert len(nl) == 1, "newline is not one token"
    69	    nl = nl[0]
    70	    cands = _candidates(tok)
    71	    cut = int(0.66 * len(cands))
    72	    id_of, pools = {}, {}
    73	    for split, src, n in (("train", cands[:cut], n_train), ("test", cands[cut:], n_test)):
    74	        chosen = []
    75	        for w, _kind in src:
    76	            if len(chosen) >= n:
    77	                break
    78	            wid = _ids(tok, " " + w)[0]
    79	            if w in id_of or wid in id_of.values():
    80	                continue
    81	            id_of[w] = wid
    82	            if _fits(tok, w, wid, [c for c in id_of if c != w], id_of, nl):
    83	                chosen.append(w)
    84	            else:
    85	                del id_of[w]
    86	        pools[split] = chosen
    87	    # Family alphabets the clock will walk by id: bare digits (Qwen splits digits, so " 1" is two tokens), single
    88	    # letters with a space, brackets. They are names by id only (no text round trip), and only in training.
    89	    for name, text in ([(d, d) for d in "0123456789"] + [(c, " " + c) for c in "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz"]
    90	                       + [(b, b) for b in "()[]"]):
    91	        ids = _ids(tok, text)
    92	        if len(ids) == 1 and ids[0] not in id_of.values() and name not in id_of:
    93	            id_of[name] = ids[0]
    94	            pools["train"].append(name)
    95	    kinds = dict(cands)
    96	    return {"train": pools["train"], "test": pools["test"], "id_of": id_of, "nl": nl,
    97	            "kinds": {s: {k: sum(kinds.get(w) == k for w in pools[s]) for k in ("lower", "cap", "code", "letter")}
    98	                      | {"extra": sum(w not in kinds for w in pools[s])} for s in ("train", "test")}}
    99

    55	def kind_of(path, expect):
    56	    path = pathlib.Path(path)
    57	    if (path / "steer.pt").is_file():
    58	        got = "e7"
    59	    elif list(path.glob("adapter_model.*")):
    60	        got = "e3"
    61	    else:
    62	        raise FileNotFoundError(f"no edit weights in {path}")
    63	    if got != expect:
    64	        raise RuntimeError(f"{path} is a {got} edit, expected {expect}")
    65	    return got

    68	def attach(model, kind, path, expect_sha=None):
    69	    path = pathlib.Path(path)
    70	    kind_of(path, kind)
    71	    loaded, undo, meta = rem_job.load_edit(model, {"kind": kind, "path": str(path)})
    72	    if expect_sha is not None and meta["sha256"] != expect_sha:
    73	        undo()
    74	        raise RuntimeError(f"sha256 {meta['sha256']} != registered {expect_sha}")
    75	    if kind == "e3":
    76	        model = loaded
    77	    return model, loaded, undo, meta

I found two reporting bugs that do not change the headline numbers: Round 1’s certification verdict ignores its required edit-hash field, and Round 2 stores some 97–98-point traces despite promising a 96-point cap. The larger issue is experimental: matched update counts do not compensate for unequal table exposure or evaluation tables selected for E-pin success.

codex
**The headline counts reproduce. The claims “forgetting is necessary” and “only pin is learnable at rank 1” do not follow.** The main problems are experimental design; I found two reporting bugs with no effect on the headline counts.

Read-only audit; no files edited. Paths below are relative to `research/remember`. Round 2’s report is `REMEMBER2.md`.

1. **Headline recount — OK**

   I ran a separate, in-memory Python script using only JSON/CSV readers. It selected jobs from each plan, required complete records, reconstructed exactness as `first_error is None or first_error >= length`, checked every JSON/CSV cell, sorted failing runs’ 1-based error steps, and selected index `(n−1)//2`. Pin counters were summed once per complete tape, excluding duplicate prefix rows.

   | Claim | Independent result | Match |
   |---|---:|---|
   | R1 pin exact at 2048 | **80/80** | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:8`, “80 of 80” |
   | R1 bitwise pin ticks | **163,840/163,840** = 80 × 2048 | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:7` |
   | R1 remember models with zero exact runs | **7/10** | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:5` |
   | R1 remember lower median first error | **3**, over **76 failures**, excluding **4 survivors** | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:6` |
   | R1 window exact at 1024 | **0/80**, zero in **10/10 models** | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:9` |
   | OLMo 5B / 84B / base, pin at 1024 | **0/4, 4/4, 4/4** | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:10` |
   | Same OLMo checkpoints, remember | **0/4, 1/4, 0/4** | Yes: `REMEMBER.md:10` |
   | R2 E-hist/remember exact at 256 | **4/16** | Yes: `REMEMBER2.md:5` |
   | R2 E-hist/remember median at 4096 | **26**, 16 failures, zero excluded | Yes: `REMEMBER2.md:6` |
   | R2 E-hist/pin exact at 4096 | **7/16** | Yes: `REMEMBER2.md:7` |
   | R2 E-pin/pin exact; E-pin/remember median | **16/16; 3** | Yes: `REMEMBER2.md:9` |
   | R2 E-pin bitwise ticks | **65,536/65,536** | Yes: `REMEMBER2.md:236` |

   “Full-history arms fail” requires qualification. The four R1 survivors are directly recorded by the substring `remember,2048,2048,1` at `results/r-qwen3-0.6b/runs.csv:41`, `:47`, `results/r-qwen3-1.7b/runs.csv:35`, and `results/r-qwen3-14b/runs.csv:29`.

   I also checked all displayed exactness cells, first-error anatomy, and margin summaries; they reproduce under the report’s **lower-median** convention. However, JSON and CSV are two serializations of the same measurements: `rem_job.py:324` explicitly says “written from the same payload as the json.” The recount validates arithmetic, not the original `torch.equal` measurements; those are performed at `rem_job.py:279`.

2. **Frozen predictions and verdicts — OK arithmetic; BUG in one guard; DESIGN FLAW in interpretation**

   The prediction sections match their registration commits (`9807f8d1` and `e9699430`). The shortened horizons are disclosed: `REMEMBER.md:108` says “Read the measured claims at 2048”; `REMEMBER2.md:76` records one tape and lengths `[256, 1024, 4096]`.

   The observations do **not** satisfy several registered predictions:

   - R1 predicted zero remember survivors in **each** model (`REMEMBER.md:65`); only 7/10 meet that condition. OLMo-84B also contradicts the zero-survivor prediction (`REMEMBER.md:71`; raw survivor at `results/r-olmo2-7b-84B/runs.csv:7`).
   - R2 predicted at least half the history runs exact at 256 in **at least three models** (`REMEMBER2.md:53`). Counts are **0/4, 1/4, 2/4, 1/4**: only Llama qualifies (`REMEMBER2.md:179`, `:183`, `:187`, `:191`).
   - R2 predicted a history median **above 32** and all E-hist/pin runs exact (`REMEMBER2.md:55`, `:57`); observed values are **26** and **7/16**. The decline from 4/16 at 256 to 0/16 at 4096 supports decline at the measured horizon, not the unmeasured 8192 prediction.

   **BUG:** R1’s registered certification guard requires “the edit file’s sha256 is in the job record” (`REMEMBER.md:127`). The actual predicate at `rem_report.py:244–246` checks certification, bitwise counts, and exactness but never checks edit metadata. Removing `edits` from an in-memory summary still returns `ruled_out=True`. Require hash presence for every counted model; compare registered hashes if claiming identity. **Current numerical effect: none**—the records contain hashes.

   **DESIGN FLAW:** Neither report implements a test of impossibility or exclusive learnability. The exactness rule is merely `return first_error is None or first_error >= length` (`rem_models.py:108`). Failure of one training recipe cannot establish that no rank-1 history solution is learnable.

3. **Context, feedback, and leakage — OK execution; DESIGN FLAW for broader claims**

   - **Exact pinned context:** tokenizer document prefix plus the **entire transition table**, followed by `[predicted current state, supplied event]`. Every table row contains its answer: `pb_pin.py:140` writes `[id_of[s], id_of[e], id_of[t], nl]`. `rem_job.py:394` prepends the document prefix; `../promptbox/pb_pin.py:243–246` uses positions `[P, P+1]` and `_clone(past)`. Thus every tick can look up its destination in Π. This is legitimate table execution, but not answer-free inference.
   - **No oracle state feedback during evaluation:** `rem_job.py:275` obtains `nxt` from the model; `:288` assigns `s = nxt`; `:290` feeds that state. `want` is used for comparison at `:282`. Round 2 does the same at `r2_eval.py:64`, `:71`, `:78–81`.
   - **Training teacher forcing is causal:** the true next state becomes the following block’s state (`r2_data.py:33–36`), while supervision is at the preceding event position (`:57`). Visibility is `idx[:, None] >= idx[None, :]` (`:82`), so that future state cannot be attended to. Training samples `pool["train"]` (`r2_train.py:155`); evaluation samples `pool["test"]` (`r2_eval.py:42`), with duplicate token IDs excluded by `../universal/uni_models.py:160`.
   - **Selection leakage/design bias:** evaluated tables were selected because E-pin already passed them: `rem_models.py:128`, `passed = [t for t in records if t["pass"]]`. Round 2 reuses that selection (`r2_models.py:88`, `return R.TABLES[name]`). This supports performance **conditional on E-pin certification**, not an unbiased comparison of the two edits’ generalization.
   - **“10 families” is inaccurate:** `rem_models.py:69–70` lists ten model checkpoints, five Qwen3 sizes. The machine generator has four shapes—`"random", "perm", "counter", "sticky"` (`../promptbox/pb_pin.py:109`)—and tables repeat across models (`rem_models.py:75–94`). These are not ten distinct machine families.
   - **A tick is one event transition**, not one bit or independent test: `rem_job.py:273–281` makes one prediction per event. Repeated visits to the same certified row contribute additional ticks.

4. **Fairness, rank, and checkpoint selection — DESIGN FLAW overall; implementation checks OK**

   **R1:** policies receive the same `pi`, tape, initial state, and weights (`rem_job.py:419–422`). Table encoding is identical; positions and execution format are not. PIN processes two tokens together at `[P,P+1]`; FULL processes individual tokens at `P+j` (`../bigidea/bi_stream.py:131`, `:140`, `:154–170`). Consequently, this comparison combines history retention, advancing positions, and forward-pass format. It does not isolate forgetting alone.

   **R2:** matching “budget” means matching supervised labels and optimizer updates. The report explicitly says “the number of tables in a step drops from 48 to 3” (`REMEMBER2.md:27`). Therefore, over 100 steps:

   - Both receive **76,800 supervised destinations**.
   - E-pin receives **4,800 table draws**; E-hist receives **300**, with correlated trajectory states.

   These are unequal data exposure and coverage, despite equal label counts. The implementations are `../universal/uni_job.py:343` for independent pinned examples and `r2_train.py:154–157` for history trajectories.

   **OK:** the actual compared recipes use 100 steps, five-step warmup, matching within-model learning rates and parameter counts. All four history records select run zero (`results2/train-*/train.json:3`, `"chosen": 0`). There is one CE loss from the first step (`r2_train.py:179–180`); checkpoint writes occur afterward (`:198–209`). There is no checkpoint selected before a delayed loss term activates. The optional retry selects by **pinned** validation (`:288–294`), which would favor pin performance, but it did not run here.

   **OK rank implementation, qualified terminology:** LoRA uses `r=1`, `lora_alpha=2`, q/k/v/o targets, and all layers (`r2_train.py:128–134`; saved config `results2/train-llama3.1-8b/edit/chosen/adapter_config.json:18`, `:20`, `:31`, `:34–38`). This is rank one **per adapted projection**, not one rank-one update for the whole network. Qwen uses an additive activation vector—`h = h + ... * add` (`../universal/uni_models.py:275`)—rather than LoRA. The report correctly distinguishes these classes at `REMEMBER2.md:21`.

5. **Figures — OK; trace-size promise — BUG**

   Both saved PNGs agree with the independently checked counts. R1 plots `exact / n` and lower-median first errors (`rem_fig.py:45`, `:55–57`). R2 plots `exact / n`, with the documented color/style mapping and E-hist/remember title counts (`r2_fig.py:30–34`, `:51`, `:57–58`), matching its caption at `REMEMBER2.md:172`.

   **BUG:** `REMEMBER2.md:35` promises “at most 96 points.” `r2_data.py:108–116` subsamples, then appends the endpoint and first-error point without enforcing the cap. Stored traces reach **97–98 points**; an example begins at `results2/eval-qwen3-1.7b/runs.json:95` and includes the appended endpoint at `:481`. Reserve slots for mandatory points before sampling. **Effect on headline numbers and these figures: none.**

**BUGs by severity**

- **Medium:** R1 certification verdict omits its required hash check; current counts unchanged.
- **Low:** R2 decimation exceeds its promised cap; current counts and figures unchanged.

**Headline claims that survive**

- **R1:** 80/80 selected, certified pin runs are recorded exact through 2048, with 163,840 matching tick logits.
- **R1 history:** 76/80 runs fail; universal failure and necessity of forgetting are unsupported.
- **R2:** this history-training recipe yields 4/16 exact at 256, 0/16 at 4096, and median first error 26.
- **R2 learnability:** “only pin is learnable at rank 1” does not survive the unequal data exposure, selected evaluation tables, and mixed edit classes.

**Minimal re-runs**

No GPU rerun is needed to retain the descriptive counts or fix the two reporting bugs. For a fair learnability comparison, retrain paired edits on identical tables, trajectories, supervised positions, steps, learning rates, and a common frozen selection rule; evaluate on fresh tables selected independently of either edit. To attribute the difference specifically to forgetting, additionally separate position reset and token-by-token versus two-token execution—the existing `TOK` and `ADV` controls already expose those distinctions (`../bigidea/bi_stream.py:156–165`).
